## Document 028: 20261023【投研報告】市場快訊_AI資金壓力拖累科技類股，惟產業需求仍具韌性

Date: 2026-10-23

AI 資金壓力拖累科技股，惟產業需求仍具韌性
2026 年 10 月 23 日
一、 市場動態
1. 費半再度重挫，科技股成為美股主要賣壓來源：美國股市主要指數 10 月 22 日跌多漲
少， 道瓊工業指數上漲0.02%，收52,867.98 點；S&P 500 指數下跌 1.21%，收7,663.43
點；Nasdaq 綜合指數下跌 2.60%，收 26,008.86 點，創 6 月 5 日以來最大單日跌幅
。費城半導體指數重挫 6.72%，收 10,731.98 點，跌幅明顯大於主要股價指數，顯示
AI 及半導體族群仍是本波市場修正的主要壓力來源。

2. AI 相關企業全面承壓 ， 資金流向非科技股及公債 ：10 月 22 日 Meta 下跌約 7%、Lam
Research 跌幅逾一成，Nvidia 下跌 4.4%，其他 AI 相關股票亦明顯走低，顯示市場
對 AI 資本支出及投資報酬的疑慮已由大型科技平台延伸至半導體與基礎建設供應商
。不過，道瓊上漲 0.02%，羅素 2000 指數亦小幅收紅，反映資金並未全面撤出股市
；美國 10 年期公債殖利率則因避險需求跌破 5%，顯示資金由高評價科技股轉向其他
產業及避險資產。
3. 中東地緣政治風險升溫，油價攀至 5 月以來高點：中東能源運輸及供給風險 10 月 22
日再度升高，布蘭特原油價格上漲約 3.76%，升至每桶 113 美元附近，創 5 月以來高
點。油價攀升除使市場重新關注能源成本對通膨與 Fed 政策路徑的影響，也可能增加
AI 資料中心的電力及營運成本。中東情勢雖非本次半導體股重挫的唯一因素，但在市
場已擔憂 AI 投資效益的情況下，能源價格上揚進一步壓抑投資人風險偏好。

二、 下跌原因分析：
1. 殖利率回落難抵科技股下跌，顯示修正因素出現轉變：10 月 14 日費半重挫主要受到
美國 9 月 CPI 高於預期、升息疑慮及美債殖利率盤中急升影響，惟當日 10 年期殖利
率收盤反而小幅下降；10 月 22 日情況則更加明顯，美國 10 年期公債殖利率因避險
需求盤中跌破 5%，費半仍大跌 6.72%。這顯示市場關注焦點已由單純的利率與估值
壓力，逐步轉向 AI 資本支出的資金來源、融資能力及投資回收期。因此，即使後續殖
利率回落，若企業仍無法證明AI 投資能轉化為足夠營收與現金流，科技股評價仍可能
持續受到壓抑。
2. Meta 自由現金流轉負 ，AI 投資成本由預期轉為實際財務壓力 ：Meta 第三季營收 646.0
億美元、年增 26%，顯示核心業務仍維持強勁成長；但資本支出達 349.1 億美元，超
過同期營業現金流 327.9 億美元，使自由現金流由去年同期正 106.3 億美元轉為負
21.2 億美元。公司亦將全年資本支出預估調整至 1,350～1,450 億美元，並宣布設立

無表決權 Class C 股票，增加未來資本運用彈性。這反映 AI 投資規模已開始超越部分

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
大型科技企業當期現金創造能力，但 Meta 仍持有 824.5 億美元現金、約當現金及有
價證券，不能將單季自由現金流轉負直接解讀為財務危機。
3. 資本稀缺逐漸成為 AI 擴張瓶頸，新興雲端業者面臨更大壓力：AI 發展需要持續投入
GPU、伺服器、資料中心及電力基礎建設，龐大資金需求使融資能力逐漸成為決定企
業成長速度的重要因素。相較具備穩定營業現金流與龐大資產規模的大型科技平台，
新興 AI 雲端服務商如 CoreWeave 及 Nebius 高度依賴外部融資，因而更容易受到借
貸成本、信用利差及投資人風險偏好變化影響。我們認為，這次修正並不代表 AI 運算

需求突然消失，而是市場開始重新評估不同企業能否負擔下一階段的擴張成本。
4. 半導體設備財報強勁卻遭賣壓，市場提高獲利兌現標準：Lam Research 於 10 月 21
日公布截至 2026 年 9 月季度（FY2027 Q1）營收 82.19 億美元，年增 54.4%、季增
22%，非 GAAP 營業利益率達 40.2%，下一季營收指引為 86 億美元、上下浮動 4 億
美元。然而，即使營運仍快速成長，10 月 22 日股價仍大幅下跌。我們研判，市場已
由過去重視 AI 訂單與營收成長，轉向同時檢視未來成長能否超越原先偏高預期，以及
AI 投資是否具備足夠的財務報酬。當股價已反映高度成長期待，即使財報表現良好，
也可能因評價調整與籌碼降溫而承壓。
5. 油價再度上漲，Fed 政策風險仍可能限制科技股評價：儘管 10 月 22 日美債殖利率因
避險需求下滑，但能源價格上漲仍可能延緩通膨降溫，使 Fed 後續政策路徑維持不確
定性。若油價持續高檔，不僅可能推升企業運輸、電力及營運成本，也將增加通膨預

期，進而限制長天期美債殖利率的下行空間。因此，科技股短線同時面臨AI 投資報酬
受到重新檢視 ， 以及能源價格與利率政策不確定的雙重壓力 。 後續10 月 28 日 FOMC
會後聲明及主席記者會，將是評估利率環境的重要依據。
三、 市場看法：
1. 股市下跌但多數個股上漲 ， 顯示賣壓集中於大型科技股 ：10 月 22 日 S&P 500 指數下
跌 1.21%，但根據美聯社（AP）報導，約三分之二成分股逆勢上漲，追蹤 S&P 500
等權重指數的 ETF（RSP）更上漲 0.73%，道瓊工業指數亦小幅收紅，呈現市值加權
指數與市場廣度明顯背離的現象 。這反映本波修正主要集中於權重較高的 AI 及大型科
技股，而非整體美股同步走弱。相較上週以防禦性類股輪動為主的情況，本週更值得
注意的是市場對高資本支出及高評價科技企業的重新定價，資金仍持續流向其他產業
，而非全面性風險撤離。只要賣壓未進一步擴散，現階段仍較接近科技股評價調整與
類股輪動，而非整體美股多頭格局反轉。
2. 台積電財測再度上修，顯示 AI 需求尚未出現結構性反轉：台積電 10 月 15 日法說會

將 2026 年美元營收成長預估由原先略高於 40%上修至約 44%， 第三季營收達新台幣
1.49 兆元、年增 50.9%，稅後純益年增 68.7%，每股盈餘 29.43 元、同樣年增 68.7%
；第四季美元營收預估為 523～543 億美元，以中間值計算季增約 14%。此外，Lam
Research 截至 9 月季度營收年增 54.4%，亦顯示半導體設備需求仍強勁。前述這些

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
數據與近期科技股重挫形成明顯對比 ， 反映目前市場主要擔憂AI 投資回收及股票評價
，而非半導體終端需求同步惡化。只要大型雲端服務商未全面縮減AI 資本支出，先進
製程與設備需求中期仍具支撐。
3. AI 投資進入報酬驗證階段，營收成長能否追上資本投入成為關鍵：我們認為，AI 產業
目前正由過去的資本支出擴張階段，逐步轉向投資報酬率與自由現金流的驗證階段。
以 Meta 為例，第三季營收仍成長 26%，但資本支出已超過營業現金流，顯示即使
AI 技術有助於改善廣告推薦及營收變現，龐大基礎建設投入仍可能使短期自由現金流

承壓。不過，資料中心及運算設備通常具有多年使用年限，不能僅以單季現金流評估
整體投資成敗。真正關鍵在於未來 AI 服務收入、營業利益及現金流成長，能否逐步抵
銷前期資本投入。具備明確商業化模式、較高資本效率及穩定現金流的企業，後續表
現可望優於高度依賴融資與成長預期的業者。
4. AI 融資風險仍集中於部分企業 ， 尚不足以認定信用危機全面擴散 ：Meta Q3 財報顯示
，該公司持有 824.5 億美元現金、約當現金及有價證券，顯示其具備一定財務緩衝能
力；相較之下，新興AI 雲端業者因資料中心建置成本龐大、現金流尚未成熟，對信用
市場與外部融資條件的敏感度相對較高 。雖然 AI 資本支出引發投資人重新評估企業信
用風險，但我們認為目前仍缺乏足夠證據認定風險已擴散至整體投資級債市場或銀行
體系。然而，後續仍宜密切觀察 AI 相關企業 CDS 與公司債利差變化、再融資能力及
主要金融機構授信條件對於科技類股及整體股債市場的後續影響。
5. 股價修正不代表企業獲利同步下修，財報表現仍有機會支撐科技股：儘管近期半導體

及 AI 相關股價回落，但台積電與Lam Research 最新財報顯示，部分供應鏈企業營收
及獲利仍維持高度成長，反映股票價格與企業基本面短期可能出現明顯落差。當市場
因融資及資本支出疑慮而降低評價倍數時，即使企業獲利持續增加，股價也可能暫時
承壓。然而，若後續大型科技公司公布的雲端營收、AI 訂單與獲利展望持續優於預期
，企業 EPS 上修仍有機會抵銷部分本益比壓縮壓力。因此，目前尚不宜將科技股價格
修正直接等同於 AI 產業景氣反轉，企業獲利能否持續兌現，仍是中期市場方向的重要
支撐。

策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
6. Fed 與大型科技股財報進入驗證期，短線震盪可能延續：Fed 將於 10 月 27～28 日舉
行利率決策會議，市場除關注是否調整政策利率外，更應重視會後聲明及主席記者會
對通膨、油價與後續利率路徑的態度。另一方面，大型雲端服務商後續公布的財報，
將提供 AI 資本支出、雲端營收及投資效率的最新證據。短線科技股仍可能受到市場評
價偏高、融資風險及地緣政治因素影響而維持震盪；不過，只要企業獲利成長與 AI
需求未出現結構性反轉，中期科技產業基本面仍具支撐，現階段維持短線審慎、中期
偏正向的看法。
四、市場展望：

1. 科技股短線宜審慎觀察 ， 中期仍看好具基本面支撐的AI 產業 ：近期科技股受到 AI 資本支
出、融資能力與評價調整等因素影響，短線波動可能延續，尤其高度依賴外部資金及獲利
尚未成熟的 AI 基礎建設業者，仍面臨較大不確定性。然而，台積電及 Lam Research 最
新財報顯示，先進製程與半導體設備需求尚未出現全面轉弱。我們認為，科技股中期展望
仍偏正向，但市場將更重視企業營收成長、訂單能見度、自由現金流及資本效率。相較缺
乏穩定獲利的 AI 概念企業，具技術優勢、財務結構穩健及獲利成長能見度的科技企業，
風險報酬相對有利。
2. AI 產業由全面成長轉向基本面分化，現金流將成為選股關鍵：隨著大型科技企業持續擴
大 AI 基礎建設投資，市場對未來投資報酬率的要求將同步提高。就目前已公布的科技股
財報結果來看，AI 產業長期成長趨勢尚未改變，但後續股價表現將更加分化，具備穩定
現金流、明確訂單及商業化能力的企業，相對有利；反之，對於高度依賴外部融資、自由
現金流持續為負且缺乏明確獲利時程的企業，仍宜維持審慎。

3. 美債避險功能重現，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：半導體類股 10/22 重挫，美國公債
價格上揚、10 年期殖利率盤中跌破 5%，顯示股市風險偏好下降時，美債仍可提供一定
避險效果。雖然 Fed 政策路徑及能源價格仍存在不確定性，但預期相較受政策利率影響
較大的短天期公債，以及受財政赤字、國債供給與期限溢價影響的超長天期公債，中天期
美國公債的風險報酬仍相對較為均衡 。 投資級債因企業信用基本面整體仍具韌性 ， 中期維
持偏正向看法；高收益債則須留意 AI 相關發行人信用風險上升及利差擴大的影響。

经 济 “压 力考 ”过 半， 政策 针对 性发 力 至关 重
要,2025年7 月15 日

消 费 表现 亮眼 ，内 外需 扰动 或加 大下 半 年经 济
压力，2025年6 月16 日

外部冲击显现，经济整体维持韧性，2025 年5
月15 日

如何看待三季度经济修复成色？

——2026 年三季度经济数据点评

◼ 三季度实际与名义 GDP 同步改善，为实现全年经济增长目标奠
定基础。今年前三季度GDP 实际增速分别为5.0%、4.3%、4.5%，
累计增长4.6%，仍在全年经济增长目标区间内。在极端天气扰
动逐步消退、节假日消费带动效应释放、AI 产业和出口景气向
上的支撑下，三季度 GDP 增速改善，较二季度提高 0.2 个百分
点，价格对名义增长支撑有所增强，名义GDP 实现6.0%的增长，
GDP 平减指数同比连续两个季度回暖。从月度数据看，9 月多
数指标环比改善，经济总体仍然延续“供给好于需求、外需优
于内需”的格局。AI 产业及出口超预期增长为工业生产、出口、
投资形成结构性支撑，新基建相关投资仍有亮点，但地产拖累
加深、项目落地偏慢、居民收入预期低迷等因素仍然对经济内
生动能形成压制，同时外部扰动和不确定加大，油价波动反复，
中东冲突对工业生产、投资影响仍未彻底消化。9 月底以来居
民购房贷款贴息、PSL 降息扩围、再贷款增额、盘活地方债结
存限额等政策接踵出台，四季度经济冲刺收官阶段，宏观政策
逆周期调节力度有望加大，预计四季度经济 将延续小幅反弹，
全年实现目标压力不大，但内生动能的修复仍 取决于结构性亮
点能否转化为更广泛的增长。 
◼ （一）三季度工业生产仍然偏强，9 月工业增加值同比回升强
于季节性，与制造业生产景气走强相互印证。季末工业生产往
往季节性回升，9 月工业生产同比增长 5.5%，较 8 月回升 0.3
个百分点，与制造业景气指标相互印证，9 月制造业 PMI 生产
指数进一步提升至 51.7%，EPMI 生产指数较上月提升 7.4 个百
分点至 54.6%，企业生产尤其是以装备制造业为主的战略性 新
兴产业的生产进一步加快。剔除季节性因素后，9 月工业增加

值季调环比（0.61%），高于过往十年平均增速 0.58%，除季末冲刺的季节性因素外，工
业生产仍然受外需偏强等因素的支撑。 
➢ 外需偏强对工业生产的支撑仍然显著 ，9 月出口相关指标均抬升至 过往较高水平。
9 月出口增速维持强势增长（36.3%），同时工业企业出口交货值增速达 17.0%，
二者增速均处于过往历史增速较高水平， 同时，以出口交货值占营收比例衡量各
行业生产的外需含量，9 月高-低出口依赖行业的工业增加值增速差为 9.3%，处于
2019 年以来高位水平，在AI 投资需求扩张、新兴国家工业化进程提速的背景下，
外需对于工业生产的支撑仍然显著。 
➢ 细分行业来看，高技术产业景气度依旧较好， 能化产业受原油上涨影响保供压力
增大。9 月高技术制造业增加值同比增长 15.3%，快于全部规模以上工业增加 值
9.8 个百分点，细分行业中， 计算机通信及电子设备、通用设备、专用设备等高
技术/装备制造业生产仍位居前列，多数增速仍维持 10%以上，高技术产业生产仍
然维持较高水平 。同时，在美伊冲突拉锯 的背景下，9 月原油价格一度再次突破
100 美元/桶，相关行业保供压力加大。9 月石油和天然气开采、煤炭开采以及化
学原料制品的生产均较 8 月抬升 2 个百分点以上，尤其是石油和天然气开采增速
达 9.8%，为近五年新高水平。生产回落仍主要集中于受需求疲软、原材料价格上
涨等多重压力影响的传统行业，如非金属矿物制品和有色金属冶炼等等。 
➢ 出口强劲也带动了服务业生产回升。9 月服务业生产指数同比增长 4.8%，较上月
回升 0.7 个百分点。分行业看，信息传输、软件和信息技术服务业、租赁和商务
服务业同比分别增9.8%和9.5%，分别较上月上涨0.2、0.5 个百分点。

3 
www.ccxi.com.cn 
如何看待三季度经济修复成色？——2026 年三季度经济数据点评

◼ （二）9 月消费边际修复更多受假期错位等季节性影响，收入、居民消费倾向偏弱等
因素对消费的掣肘仍然存在 。综合商品与服务消费看，前三季度社会消费商品和服务
零售总额同比增长 2.6%，较 1-8 月略有回升（0.1 个百分点）。9 月单月社零同比回
升 0.7 个百分点至 1.1%，考虑到今年中秋假期提前至 9 月，假期错位影响下，出游相
关消费如餐饮（同比增长 2%）的释放或对同比读数形成一定的支撑。但在剔除季节性
因素后，9 月社零环比增长0.35%，仍然低于过去十年的平均水平（0.5%）。尤其是前
三季度居民名义可支配收入增速仅为 5.3%，已经连续三个季度落后于名义 GDP 增长，
金融数据中新增居民短贷连续三个月同比 下降，同时居民人均消费支出增速（2.7%）
与人均可支配收入增速（4.4%）的差值进一步扩大， 这意味着除了以旧换新政策的需
求预支效应等影响外，收入以及消费倾向对居民消费掣肘持续存在。 
➢ 9 月商品零售更多受节庆效应支撑，必选消费表现靠前 ，补贴类商品中仅有换机
窗口的通讯器材维持较高增长 。拆分商品消费的细分表现看 ，9 月五大补贴类商
品的加权社零增速为-6.3%，较上月整体持平，其中，仅换新周期较短的通讯器材
和文化办公用品（电脑及配件）表现相对较好，尤其 9 月为各大手机厂商新机陆
续发布的窗口期，通讯器材社零增长超过 30%，远超汽车、家电、家具等其他补
贴品类的消费表现。非补贴类商品的加权社零增速回升近 3.5 个百分点至 3.6%，
是 9 月商品消费的主要支撑项。其中，粮油食品、饮料、日用品增速维持韧性增
长，原油制品、黄金消费跌幅分别收窄 6.2、7.9 个百分点更多受油价上涨的名义
因素及基数的支撑。

➢ 服务零售边际回暖，与高频指标 较为一致。中秋假期出游的支撑下， 9 月服务零
售同比回升 0.1 个百分点至 5.0%，服务业 PMI 从 49.3%回升至 50.2%。从高频数
据来看，9 月出行和餐饮消费均出现边际好转，27 城地铁客运量增速由 8 月的-1.8%
回升至 9 月的 1.5%，商务部数据的中秋假期全国重点零售和餐饮企业日均销售额
比去年农历同期增长 6.1%，商务部重点监测的 78 个步行街营业额同比增长 5.1%，
较端午假期的 3.5%有所改善。

业投资是三大投资的 主要改善项，民间投资跌幅仍在走阔 。1-9 月固定资产投资同比

和二手房同比跌幅均有收窄，但环比来看仅有一线城市 的商品房、二手房价格 延
续上涨，背后仍受核心地段的改善性需求支撑。地产开发链条依然全线承压，1-9
月地产投资较前 8 月进一步下滑 0.7 个百分点至-20.6%，同时到位资金下降 21.6%，
跌幅均创历史新高 ，行业整体资本开支意愿偏弱，在地产库存高企、房企经营承
压的背景下，房企普遍回避需求基本面偏弱、去化压力高企的区域， 地产投资或
仍处于收缩态势。 
➢ 基建投资跌幅继续扩大，资金下放及项目落地进展偏慢仍是主要制约，但结构上
六张网等新基建的投资拉动显现。前三季度基建同比较前 8 月回落 0.2 个百分点至
-4.2%，已连续四个月为负。就下滑原因看，资金发行节奏和项目落地进度偏缓仍
是主要原因。据统计，今年广义财政支出进度、专项债发行进展和准财政落地速
度整体均偏慢，8 月广义财政支出累计、当月同比下滑 3.5%、6.7%，同时，1-9 月
新增专项债发行进度为全年的 81%，低于去年 83.2%的同时也低于过去三年平均
水平 86.8%，8000 亿新型政策性金融工具下放在下半年陆续下放，进展上也低于
年初的预期； 但从委托贷款数据看，新型政策性金融工具 正在起效，9 月委托贷
款同比多增 519 亿元，预计四季度基建投资降幅将明显收敛。此外，项目端仍然
是制约固定资产投资的主要约束，1-8 月施工项目计划总投资同比-5.3%，较 1-7 月
的-4.5%继续回落；新开工项目计划总投资同比 -22.3%，整体延续走弱。同时，化
债压力、资金错配、项目储备不足、地方对投资项目的审查趋于审慎等深层次原
因的制约持续存在。 值得注意的是， 从细分行业看， 9 月基建投资中信息传输业
投资增长 30.8%、航空、水上运输业投资也实现 10%以上的增长，来自六张网、
智慧交通等融合基础设施的新基建仍然能够形成一定的投资增量 ，当前投资总量
虽然有所下滑，但结构调整、优化的进程也在加快。  
➢ 制造业投资 仍然向少数高景气行业集中， 地缘冲突反复、供给冲击及价格分化压
力是制约制造业投资的主要原因。1-9 月制造业投资同比增速小幅回升 0.2 个百分
点至-2.1%，但整体仍然偏弱，地缘扰动反复、油价中枢再度回升的背景下，多数
受影响行业环节仍选择观望并放缓投资节奏。结构上，装备制造业及 AI 产业链投
资仍是主要拉动项 ，需求偏弱下价格分化压力也会对中下游制造业带来一定的挤
出，受成本上升影响 中下游制造业 （有色金属冶炼加工、金属制品、食品制造等）
投资整体跌幅仍然较大。

如何看待三季度经济修复成色？——2026 年三季度经济数据点评

不大，但经济内生动能的修复需要政策积极发力 。后续来看，出口和高技术产业仍能
为全年生产、投资、出口等各项经济指标形成支撑 ，三季度围绕财政、地产 等各项增

展望未來，南亞科(2408.TT) 因受限於極低庫存，2027 年上半年
位元出貨量預期將保持平穩，待 4Q27 5A 新廠開出產能後，出貨
量將恢復逐步成長；產能方面，5A 新廠規劃於 2027 年第一季開
始進場裝機，第一階段預計 2028 年底前投片量達 3 萬片，全產
能 5 萬片目標於 2030 年底達成，且擴建嚴格以客戶 LTA 的 AI
專案為導向；此外，公司規劃於屏東科學園區建置3D 晶圓封裝與
測試廠，預計 2028 下半年導入設備，並與客戶合作開發客製化  
AI/WoW(晶圓堆疊)與 ASIC 專案，已開始產生部分營收貢獻，長
期目標於 2029 至 2030 年將 AI 基礎設施營收占比拉高至 40%
至 50%；技術面上，次世代 1C 製程預計於 2027 年第一季進行
客戶驗證 。 綜合以上 ， 研究部預估南亞科2027 年 EPS 為 130.72
元。考量目前公司記憶體供給吃緊、需求擴大，有長期合約支持未
來成長 ，且公司具備獨特製程升級與客製化產品線成長潛力 ， 研究
部給予 5.5 倍本益比評價，目標價 710 元，投資建議為買進。

⚫ 公司基本資料 南亞科(2408.TT)公司簡介 
南亞科技(2408.TT)成立於 1995 年，隸屬於台塑集團，為全球領先的動
態隨機存取記憶體(DRAM)製造大廠 。 公司生產基地主要位於台灣 ， 擁有
多樣化的產品組合，涵蓋 DDR5、LPDDR5/5X、DDR4、LPDDR4/4X、
DDR3 及 LPDDR3 等產品線 ， 產品種類達數十顆 ， 服務全球數百家客戶 。
面對傳統記憶體產業之劇烈循環波動，公司近年積極推動技術升級與結
構性轉型，除中長期積極投入 1B(16 奈米)與 1C(12 奈米)先進製程轉換
及新五廠建置，並深入布局企業級 SSD(eSSD)、SmartNIC、BMC 等
AI 伺服器基礎設施，同時與客戶合作開發客製化 AI/WoW(晶圓堆疊)利
基產品，正逐步由傳統大宗商品化DRAM 廠，轉型為具備高附加價值與
高敏捷度的全方位利基型記憶體解決方案供應商 。

2Q26 營運表現全面成長，售價調漲驅動獲利大幅提升 
南 亞 科 (2408.TT) 2Q26 營 業 收 入 為 825.49 億元 (Q+68.17% / 
Y+684.23%) ， 營收展現強勁成長 ， 受惠於平均銷售單價(ASP)季增超過
60%的漲勢，有效抵消了位元出貨量與上季持平的量能限制；而在 ASP 
大幅上漲且成本結構維持穩定的推升下，單季毛利率提升至
79.49%(Q+11.60ppt / Y+100.05ppt) 。雖然員工分紅與銷售研發使營業

3Q26 獲利大幅跳升，低價長約重簽驅動 ASP 季增逾六成

2026 年受惠 DRAM 報價持續上漲與 AI 伺服器需求，獲利創歷史新高

位數百分比的目標；合約與定價機制上，公司目前長約(LTA)覆蓋率落在 
50% 至 70% 之間，其中 3 年期以上長約占總出貨量 20% 以上，履
約率高達 100%，預期第四季 DRAM 供需緊缺狀態延續，合約平均售
價保持溫和成長；需求端方面，GPU、TPU、CPU 及 ASIC 等 AI 基
礎建設建置強勁，推升 AI 伺服器與基礎設施相關產品之營收占比突破 
20%； 產品組合中 DDR4 與 LPDDR4 合計營收占比超過 60%，DDR5 
占比約 10%。管理層指引目前產業短期內難以緩解供給壓力，隨著第四
季 DRAM ASP 持續看漲 ， 公司毛利率表現有機會再進一步拉升 。 綜合以
上，研究部預估南亞科(2408.TT) 2026 年營業收入為 4,190.95 億元，
Y+529.40%、毛利率  82.61%，Y+60.10ppt、營業利益率  76.83%，

長鑫存儲(CXMT)技術進展與產業影響 
長鑫存儲(CXMT)於 2026 年 9 月宣佈第五代 DRAM 制程(G5)進入量產階
段。儘管目前 G5 產品仍處於客戶驗證與良率提升階段，距離大規模商
業化出貨尚需時間 ， 但已顯示中國DRAM 技術發展速度明顯超出市場原
先預期。 長鑫 HBM3E 良率已達 40%左右。市場普遍認為，長鑫在無法
取得 EUV 設備的情況下，主要透過 ArF DUV 浸潤式曝光搭配多重曝光
(Multiple Patterning) 技術推進制程微縮，因此雖然能夠達到接近國際大
廠先進 DRAM 節點的設計能力 ， 但製造成本 、 良率與生產效率仍與三星 、
SK 海力士及美光存在差距。未來能否持續提升至國際大廠 70%以上的
量產水準，將是觀察長鑫HBM 競爭力的重要指標。短期而言，長鑫技術
進步對台廠商影響有限，長鑫目前仍面臨良率提升與客戶驗證所需時
間，短期內難以快速增加有效供給，因此全球 DRAM 供應緊張的局面仍
將延續至 2027 至 2028 年。相較之下，三星、SK 海力士與美光持續將
產能轉向 HBM 產品，反而造成 DDR4 供給逐漸退出市場，進一步支撐
DDR4 價格維持強勢，有利於以 DDR4 產品為主的南亞科營運表現；然
而，中長期則需注意，長鑫 DRAM 投片量將於 2027 年底達到約 30K 
WPM，並持續擴產，若未來 G5 良率持續改善、HBM 技術逐步成熟，
加上中國市場本土化政策支持，長鑫未來在中低階 DDR5、PC DRAM
及中國本土市場的競爭力可能快速提升 ， 進而對全球DRAM 供需格局產
生實質影響。

長鑫存儲(CXMT)4F²技術 
長鑫存儲在 10 月 12 日有表示年底推出 4F²架構 DDR5 伺服器記憶體，
4F²是將每個記憶體儲存單元配置在面積相當於 4 倍 F 平方的區域內 ， 其
中 F 代表半導體制程的最小線寬，這種設計能讓同一顆晶片容納更多儲
存單元，進而提高記憶體容量。目前，主流商用 DRAM 仍採用 6F 平方
儲存單元結構。長鑫存儲表示，其4F 平方設計的核心，在於自主研發的
垂直環繞閘極(Vertical Surrounding Gate，VSG)電晶體，這項技術透過
垂直閘極架構改善漏電問題，同時大幅縮小單元面積，提高儲存密度。
SK 海力士(SK Hynix) 已於 2025 年公佈 4F 平方技術發展藍圖，另外，
根據南韓媒體今年 4 月的報導，三星電子(Samsung Electronics)計畫於
2028 年前，量產採用這項新設計、制程低於 10 奈米的 DRAM。

結論與建議 
展望未來 ， 南亞科(2408.TT) 因受限於極低庫存 ，2027 年上半年位元出
貨量預期將保持平穩 ， 待4Q27 5A 新廠開出產能後 ， 出貨量將恢復逐步
成長；產能方面，5A 新廠規劃於 2027 年第一季開始進場裝機，第一

評價，目標價 710 元，投資建議為買進。

### Page 6

CHPT (6510.TWO)
28 October 2026 Citi Research
6
CHPT
Company description
Chunghwa Precision Test Tech (CHPT), established in 2005, is a global leader
in semiconductor test interfaces. Historically focused on the smartphone AP
segment, CHPT has successfully pivoted its strategic focus towards high-
growth AI/HPC applications. Utilizing its unique "All-in-House" vertical
integration model and leading mechanical design & simulation technology,
the company specializes in probe cards, probe card PCBs, and load boards for

top-tier chipmakers. This comprehensive portfolio effectively addresses
high-frequency signal bottlenecks, providing one-stop-shop solutions that
ensure structural reliability across the global AI computing supply chain.
Investment strategy
We rate CHPT at Buy/High Risk (1H). We believe CHPT is well positioned for
the AI infrastructure cycle, with leadership supported by its unique "All-in-
House" vertical integration model and broad participation across next-gen AI

GPU and US AI ASIC projects. Its technical high ground in advanced nodes
solutions and dedicated localized support provide it a defensible position, in
our view, as advanced packaging demand expands. The company captures a
wider share of the test interface value chain across probe PCBs, load boards,
and high-end burn-in boards, providing a multi-layered earnings engine.
Valuation
We value CHPT using a PE-based approach that factors in earnings
momentum and the structural shift towards high-end testing. Our target

price of NT$4,000 is derived from applying 35x to 2027E EPS, implying the
company's average forward PE since 2024 +1 SD, which we believe is justified
given the company's entrenched position in the 2nm/3nm testing segment,
rising contribution from US AI GPU & AI ASIC projects, and the unique
competitive advantage of its "All-in-House" vertical integration model. With
these structural drivers in place, and as we believe the market is starting to
value it as an AI/HPC play (rather than a consumer electronics stock), we

expect the multiple to hold at a premium relative to its historical averages.
Risks
Our quant system rates the CHPT stock as High Risk for its share price
volatility.
Key downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target
price, and to our earnings forecasts, include: 1) margin compression from
lower-than-expected utilization rates; 2) execution delays in concentrated
US AI GPU and ASIC programs; 3) intensifying competition in testing boards

from global peers; and 4) structural gross margin dilution from a shift towards
high-end burn-in boards.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

營收走勢圖

電子–資訊服務 
藍新資訊(6938.TT) 
維運收入堆疊，AI 與資安接棒成長

研究部初次給予藍新資訊買進投資評等 ， 主要考量 ：(1)企業 AI 應用由
概念驗證走向落地，加上資安設備屆期汰換與金融業大型主機換機，專
案建置需求延續至 2027 年；(2)建置案驗收後轉入長期維護維運 ， 持續
性收入逐年堆疊，墊高獲利基礎；(3)上櫃現金增資擴大資本規模，承接
億元級大型專案的能力提升。綜上所述，研究部預估藍新資訊  2026、
2027 年營收分別為 8.33 億元與 9.55 億元，分別成長 23.1% 與 
14.7% YoY；稅後 EPS 分別為 7.38 元與 8.39 元，分別成長 23.9% 與 
28.3% YoY。藍新資訊以核心系統維運掌握客戶升級需求，並以約九成盈
餘發放現金股利，以 10/16 興櫃收盤價 80.8 元計算，2026 年現金殖
利率約 7.2%。 採樣資訊服務類股本益比介於 10～20 倍 ， 考量藍新資訊 
2026、2027 年獲利維持雙位數成長，以 2027 年 EPS 為評價基礎，給
予區間中緣的 15 倍本益比，目標價 125 元，評等為買進。

1. 核心系統技術門檻高，客戶黏著度強 
藍新資訊以 IBM UNIX 大型主機起家 ， 承接金融核心交易與企業 ERP 系
統，國內具備同等能力的業者有限；建置與資料搬遷不委外原廠，使毛
利率高於一般設備經銷商，主要客戶合作多逾十年。

2. 建置帶動維運，持續性收入逐年堆疊 
核心系統驗收並度過保固期後，客戶多續簽一至三年以上的維護合約，
存續率逾九成 ， 維運收入因此逐年堆疊 ，2025 年以近四成營收貢獻逾五
成毛利 。 研究部預估整合技術與服務 2026、2027 年營收分別成長 13.4% 
與 14.5% YoY。

3. AI 落地與換機需求，上櫃後承接大型專案 
企業 AI 應用由概念驗證走向落地，帶動 AI 相關營收占比由 2025 年
約 3% 升至約 8%，加上資安與大型主機換機，專案需求延續至 2027 
年。上櫃後單案規模可望擴大至 1～2 億元，研究部預估商品銷售與系
統整合合計 2026、2027 年營收分別成長 29.4% 與 14.8% YoY。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要披露及免責聲明 2

公司簡介 
藍新資訊成立於 2014 年，由藍新科技的系統整合事業群分割獨立，2018 年
起獲精誠資訊策略性投資，目前精誠持股約 48%。藍新資訊為資訊服務產業
的系統整合商，向上透過加值代理商取得 IBM、HPE、Dell、Cisco、NetApp、
VMware、Palo Alto 等國際原廠軟硬體，向下直接服務政府機關、金融機構與
大型企業。藍新資訊以 IBM Power 等 UNIX 大型主機平台起家，長期承接企
業 ERP、MES、HIS 與金融核心交易系統的建置與維運，並延伸至儲存備援、
網路資安與 AI 算力平台。

產品結構 
藍新資訊的營收分為商品銷售 、 系統整合 、 整合技術與服務三大業務 。 商品銷
售與系統整合屬專案性收入，提供伺服器、儲存、網路與資安設備的銷售、規
劃與安裝建置，2025 年營收占比分別為 10.3% 與 50.7%；依應用區分，雲
端基礎建設近五成，數據儲存、數位韌性與 AI 應用平台約 25～35%，資訊
安全與網路約 17～25%。整合技術與服務為保固期滿後的例行維護、SLA 支
援、高階維運與人力派駐，屬持續性收入，2025 年營收占比 38.8%，貢獻約 
54% 的毛利 。 另有少量機臺租賃收入 （占比 0.2%） ， 圖一併入商品銷售計算。

圖一、2024～2027F 藍新資訊各業務營收比重

資料來源：藍新資訊、統一投顧預估

藍新資訊為系統整合商，以 UNIX 
大型主機技術切入政府、金融與
大型企業的核心系統，並延伸至
儲存備援、網路資安與 AI 算力
平台。

整合技術與服務以  38.8% 的營
收占比貢獻約 54% 毛利，是藍新
資訊的獲利核心。

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要披露及免責聲明 3

商業模式 
藍新資訊以專案建置取得客戶核心系統，再以長期維護維運把一次性收入轉
為持續性收入 。 專案完成驗收並度過保固期後 ， 客戶多延續簽訂一至三年以上
的維護合約 ； 核心系統對穩定度要求高 ， 更換服務商的風險與成本也高 ，近三
年 平 均 維 護 合 約 存 續 率 逾 九 成 ， 合 約 件 數 2023 ～2025 年複合成長率  
14.48%。 維運過程掌握客戶的 IT 環境 ， 又帶出設備汰換 、 架構升級與資安強
化的新專案 ， 形成建置帶動維運 、 維運帶動升級的循環。 整合技術與服務以同
一組技術團隊服務更多設備與合約 ， 人力成本相對固定 ， 營收占比由 2024 年
的 33.0% 提升至 2025 年的 38.8%，毛利率由 40.7% 升至 43.1%，帶動整
體毛利率由 2024 年的 28.1% 升至 2025 年的 31.0%。

圖二、藍新資訊由專案導入延伸至長期維運

資料來源：藍新資訊，統一投顧整理

營收季節性 
藍新資訊營收具季節性 ， 政府機關與企業多於年底集中驗收結案 ， 第四季為全
年高峰，2023～2025 年第四季營收分別占全年 42.7%、52.2% 與 41.2%，12 
月單月即占全年 25～31%。商品銷售與系統整合於驗收時點認列，單季營收
隨大型專案進度波動 ； 整合技術與服務依合約期間認列 ， 可平滑專案收入的波
動。2026 年 1～9 月營收 5.06 億元（+27.2% YoY），研究部預估 4Q26 營
收 3.26 億元，占全年 39.2%。

建置帶動維運 、 維運帶動升級 ， 維
護合約存續率逾九成，持續性收
入逐年堆疊並推升整體毛利率。

年底驗收結案集中，第四季營收
占全年四至五成；維運收入依合
約認列，平滑專案收入的波動。

### Page 4

請參見末頁重要披露及免責聲明 4

圖三、2023～2026F 藍新資訊各季營收

資料來源：藍新資訊、統一投顧預估

客戶結構與技術能量 
藍新資訊累計服務客戶逾 400 家，營收約 50% 來自民營大型企業，政府機
關約 40%，金融機構約 10%，客戶包括行政院所屬部會、金控與證券業，以及
高科技與製造業龍頭，主要客戶合作多逾十年。客戶分散，2025 年前十大客
戶合計占營收約 35%，單一最大客戶占 7.0%；1Q26 因數位轉型再造建置案
第一階段驗收，單一客戶占比升至 14.3%，屬專案認列時點造成的短期集中。
藍新資訊員工約 135 人，其中技術人員 86 人，累計取得 642 張專業證照，
其中雲端運算、AI 與大數據、資訊安全與  IBM 大型主機四大領域合計占  
93%。建置與資料搬遷由內部團隊執行、不委外原廠，可自行完成  SAP 與 
Oracle ERP 的跨平台移轉，是毛利率高於一般設備經銷商的主因。

AI 應用與資訊安全布局 
藍新資訊成立 AI 智能發展部，提供 GPU 算力平台、向量資料庫、RAG 知識
庫 、 企業私有化大型語言模型部署與資料治理 ， 並以駐點輔導與教育訓練協助
客戶落地，以既有逾 400 家客戶為交叉銷售基礎。單純銷售設備的毛利率有
限 ， 藍新資訊以自有技術團隊提供資料治理 、系統整合與後續維運 ， 聚焦整合
服務而非設備銷售 。 資安業務不與專業資安廠商正面競爭 ， 而是從既有維運客
戶切入，承接防火牆汰換、網路架構升級、零信任身分與存取控管，以及雙活
資料中心與災難備援。上櫃現金增資後，藍新資訊已開始承接 8,000 萬元以
上的案件，未來可承接 1～2 億元等級的大型專案，較過去單案多在 5,000
～6,000 萬元以下明顯擴大。

客戶逾 400 家且分散，建置與資
料搬遷不委外，是藍新資訊毛利
率高於一般設備經銷商的主因。

AI 由伺服器銷售延伸至資料治
理與應用落地，以整合服務支撐
毛利率；資安則從維運切入換機
商機。

### Page 5

請參見末頁重要披露及免責聲明 5

產業分析 
台灣資訊服務市場 
依資策會 MIC 統計與預估，台灣系統整合市場 2023～2027 年複合成長率約 
7.8%，資訊委外市場則達 21.1%，主因企業 IT 架構日趨複雜、資訊人才短
缺，基礎設施維運與資安逐步交由專業廠商承接；台灣資安產值 2025～2027 
年亦可望維持雙位數成長 。 研究部認為委外與資安成長高於系統整合 ， 有利維
運收入占比近四成、並以維運切入資安換機的藍新資訊。

AI 落地與資安換機推升系統整合需求 
企業 AI 應用由概念驗證走向落地，除採購 GPU 伺服器，還需搭配儲存、高
速網路 、 容器平台 、 資料治理與權限控管 ， 導入 AI Agent 後亦須與既有 ERP、
人資及核心交易系統串接；台灣企業的研發與財務等敏感資料多傾向留在自
有機房，私有化 AI 部署更仰賴具備底層整合能力的系統整合商。AI 普及同
時提高資料外洩風險 ， 老舊網路與資安設備又陸續停止原廠支援 ， 政府與企業
須換機並導入零信任架構與雙活備援。核心系統亦進入換機期，金融業  IBM 
大型主機進入汰換階段，企業 ERP 平台也因效能與資安規範展開新一輪升
級。研究部認為AI 落地、資安換機與核心系統汰換同時發生，加上 IT 人才
短缺使企業提高維運委外比重，有利兼具建置與維運能力的系統整合商。

營運概況 
1. AI 基礎設施建置帶動 2Q26 營收年增逾三成 
企業加速建置基礎設施帶動 2Q26 營收達 1.85 億元（+27.3% QoQ 、+32.6% 
YoY） 。惟記憶體與關鍵零組件漲價推升設備採購成本，毛利率降至 29.2%（-
2.0ppt QoQ、-0.8ppt YoY），營益率 12.9%；稅後淨利 1,930 萬元（+5.3% 
QoQ、+118.3% YoY），EPS 1.54 元。

2. 年底驗收旺季帶動 2026 年營收年增逾兩成 
3Q26 營收 1.77 億元（-4.1% QoQ 、+38.2% YoY ），1～9 月累計營收年增 
27.2%。年底為政府與企業驗收結案高峰，政府預算解凍後標案陸續執行，預
估 4Q26 營收 3.26 億元（+84.3% QoQ、+17.1% YoY），惟大型建置案比重
提高，毛利率降至 26.6%，EPS 2.72 元。預估 2026 年營收 8.33 億元
（+23.1% YoY），EPS 7.38 元。

3. AI 與資安需求延續，2027 年營收維持雙位數成長 
展望 2027 年，企業 AI 應用由概念驗證走向落地，AI 相關營收占比已由 
2025 年約 3% 提升至約 8%，部分客戶的 AI 案件已進入概念驗證，並著手
編列 2027 年預算；老舊網路與資安設備陸續停止原廠支援，加上金融業大
型主機進入汰換期，換機需求延續，部分大型專案的認列亦延續至 2027 年。
預估 2027 年營收 9.55 億元 （+14.7% YoY） ，毛利率 29.6%，EPS 8.39 元。

台灣資訊委外與資安市場成長高
於系統整合，有利維運收入占比
近四成的藍新資訊。

AI 落地、資安換機與核心系統汰
換同時發生，系統整合商由銷售
設備轉向提供整體架構與長期維
運。

基礎設施建置帶動 2Q26 營收年
增 32.6%，惟記憶體與零組件漲
價使毛利率降至 29.2%。

年底 驗收旺季加上政府預算解
凍 ， 預估 4Q26 營收年增 17.1%，
2026 年 EPS 7.38 元。

資安與大型主機換機需求延續，
預估 2027 年營收年增 14.7%，
EPS 8.39 元。

### Page 6

請參見末頁重要披露及免責聲明 6

投資建議 
研究部初次給予藍新資訊買進投資評等，主要考量：(1)企業 AI 應用由概念
驗證走向落地 ， 加上資安設備屆期汰換與金融業大型主機換機 ， 專案建置需求
延續至 2027 年；(2)建置案驗收後轉入長期維護維運 ， 持續性收入逐年堆疊 ，
墊高獲利基礎；(3)上櫃現金增資擴大資本規模，承接億元級大型專案的能力
提升。綜上所述，研究部預估藍新資訊 2026、2027 年營收分別為 8.33 億
元與 9.55 億元，分別成長 23.1% 與 14.7% YoY ；稅後 EPS 分別為 7.38 
元與 8.39 元，分別成長 23.9% 與 28.3% YoY。藍新資訊以核心系統維運掌
握客戶升級需求 ， 並以約九成盈餘發放現金股利 ， 以 10/16 興櫃收盤價 80.8 
元計算，2026 年現金殖利率約 7.2%。採樣資訊服務類股本益比介於 10～20 
倍，考量藍新資訊 2026、2027 年獲利維持雙位數成長，以 2027 年 EPS 為
評價基礎，給予區間中緣的 15 倍本益比，目標價 125 元，評等為買進。

Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The U.S. Capex Boom No One is Talking About—And Its Implications
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
We have long been of the belief that the U.S. is on the cusp of an inbound FDI capex boom
that can result in numerous positive spillover effects for the U.S. economy. The latest

figures from the U.S. Department of Commerce bear this out.
Exhibit 1 highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be inside the U.S.,
with FDI totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year. That is a staggering
103% jump from a year ago and places FDI inflows on track for a record year.
The latest inbound capex already adds to the massive scale of foreign investment in the
U.S. To wit, the accumulated stock of FDI in the U.S. stands at nearly $6T, based on the

latest figures from the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA). Manufacturing accounts for
roughly 43% of the total, and, at last count, some 10,000 foreign affiliates were operating
in the U.S.
These firms are largely invisible to the average U.S. investor, but the activities of U.S.
affiliates of foreign multinationals are substantial and competitive- and growth-enhancing.
To this point and based on the latest data from the BEA, foreign affiliates in the U.S.

employed nearly 8.6 million U.S. workers in 2024. That represents 6.1% of total private
sector employment, with manufacturing and retail the two most prominent sectors
of employment. Roughly 24% of all manufacturing jobs in the U.S. are from foreign
subsidiaries. In terms of pay, U.S. workers at these firms make $89,000 annually, 7% higher
than the national average, according to the Global Business Alliance.
Foreign affiliates also contributed some $1.5T to U.S. GDP; comprised some 15.7% of total

U.S. private sector investment; accounted for 12.4% of total U.S. research & development
(R&D) expenditures; paid roughly 20% of all U.S. federal taxes; and made up 22.5% of
U.S. exports and 26.2% of U.S. imports. 1 These are the direct effects of U.S. subsidiaries
of foreign companies operating in the U.S. The indirect effects are even greater in terms
of more income, jobs, growth and tax revenue—all bolstering the long-term competitive
advantage of the U.S. In a nutshell, foreign firms operating in the U.S. are an important

ingredient of U.S. economic growth.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Companies that once served the U.S. market exclusively through exports
increasingly face higher costs and more uncertainties and hence want to be “inside.”
Two, with a $32T-plus economy with extraordinary consumer purchasing power, foreign

firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment than just trade (exports). There
is no more dynamic consumer in the world than the American shopper.
Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers, semiconductors, power
generation, etc., has created numerous investment opportunities for many foreign firms
that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging capex cycle.
Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly developed

technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.
As more U.S. affiliates of foreign multinationals set up shop in the U.S., think more job
creation across the country, more manufacturing output, more locally sourced purchases,
more U.S. exports, more spending on R&D, and more tax revenue for local communities.
And over the long run, think a more globally competitive U.S. economy.
1 Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign Multinational Enterprises, 2024” and “Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign

Multinational Enterprises, 2023”, BEA July 2026.
Investment Implications
The boom in foreign investment in
the U.S. should continue to support
employment, productivity, corporate
earnings, and broader U.S. economic
competitiveness, in our view. Hence
our U.S. bias.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
And speaking of global competitiveness, Exhibit 2A is very illustrative of the magnet pull of
the U.S. economy to attract FDI versus the rest of the world. Of new greenfield investment
this decade, note the overriding preference for the U.S., accounting for nearly 16% of total
global greenfield investment this decade. America’s share is larger than the next three
countries combined—the United Kingdom, India, and Germany. And note that America’s

share of new investment is more than 5X that of China’s. Most of this new investment
has gone into manufacturing sectors like electrical equipment, computers, appliances and
batteries and has originated from Asia and Europe.
The bulk of foreign investment comes from Europe, followed by Japan and Canada, but as
Exhibit 2B highlights, there is a new dynamic playing out when it comes to America’s ability
to attract capital. There is a new source in town: a handful of developing nations (namely

Taiwan, South Korea, Mexico and Brazil). Mexican manufacturers, Taiwan technology
leaders, South Korea chip leaders, Brazil industrial firms, India pharmaceutical firms—these
companies and more are realizing that in today’s trade environment, the best way to serve
the American market is not necessarily through exports but through investment—investing
locally. FDI flows from developing nations reached $63B in the 12 months through June,
more than double the level a year prior ($24.5B), according to the BEA. Fueled by insatiable

AI demand, flows from South Korea alone totaled $23.4B in the first half of 2026. The
upshot is that an increasingly important source of capital for the U.S. is coming from an
unlikely source: the developing nations.
More fire for the capex boom. The coming boom in FDI inflows is supportive of our thesis
that the U.S. stands on the cusp of a major industrial upgrade. We are in the early innings
of a structural—not cyclical—capex cycle that should lead to the rebuilding of domestic

supply chains, higher value-added manufacturing, and more AI-driven productivity growth.
Foreign firms, in our opinion, will be part of this process.
That is important to recognize because remember the following: when a foreign company
builds a factory or an R&D facility or expands its existing manufacturing capacity right
here in the U.S., good things happen. Demand for U.S. goods and services goes up (as does
demand for construction, materials, logistics and professional services). New jobs are created,

boosting incomes of U.S. workers, while supporting more local spending and consumption.
And because many foreign firms often bring advanced manufacturing technologies when
investing in the U.S., the end results include a boost in productivity and innovation in various
sectors of the economy.
All the above, of course, are the secret ingredients to stronger U.S. economic growth, rising
corporate earnings and higher equity prices. With a little help from our friends, America’s

industrial renewal is gaining strength and momentum.

MARKET VIEW
Europe’s Electoral Cycle and Market Risks
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
As midterm elections approach for the U.S. next month, voters across Europe will also
enter their own election season starting this November and continuing throughout
2027. Over the next year, 52% of the total EU population, representing 46% of EU GDP
across eight countries, will go to the polls, including voters in three of its four largest

economies (Exhibit 3). Having announced snap general elections earlier this month,
Spain will vote for members of its lower and upper house on November 29. A series of
parliamentary elections are then due for 2027 in Estonia (by March 7), Finland (on April
18), Greece (by July 25), Slovakia (by September 28), Poland (by November 11) and Italy
(by December 22). And perhaps most consequentially France will stage Presidential
elections on April 18, with a potential second round of voting set for May 2.

For the EU as a whole, the significance of these coming elections will be the potential
for a shift toward more populist, domestically oriented policy priorities from leading
opposition candidates (particularly in France, Spain and Poland) which could obstruct the
process of closer EU integration. In the nearer term, adoption of the new seven-year
(2028-2034) EU budget due by the end of 2027 could be impeded. And over the longer
term, renewed efforts toward an EU-wide capital market union to deepen and attract

more inward investment into European markets could also be further delayed.
At the country level, investors have seen bond yields rise globally in 2026 on the back of
higher inflation and concerns over fiscal sustainability. The debt question has arguably
been most pressing in parts of Europe, especially given the likelihood that upcoming
elections make for domestic increases in government largesse. And of the eight
countries going to the polls over the next year, France remains the biggest source of

concern. Interest rate spreads on French 10-year sovereign bonds have overtaken those
in both Greece and Italy this year (Exhibit 4), driven by investor concerns over public
debt. The 2027 government budget unveiled earlier this month projected a deficit of
5.0% of GDP (well in excess of the 3% specified in the EU Stability and Growth Pact),
but remained insufficiently expansive to nullify the risk that the current government
could be unseated later this year if it is forced through the fragmented parliament. This

comes on top of an existing debt load of close to 120% of GDP and a halving of official
2026 growth forecasts from 1.0% at the start of the year to 0.5% in September. In
short, all four variables in France’s debt sustainability equation currently stand as major
headwinds, and the two leading candidates in the 2027 election have shown limited
appetite for fiscal consolidation over the coming years.

Investment Implications
We remain cautious on European
markets overall, despite seeing
pockets of strength in selected
areas such as defense, data center
infrastructure and renewable energy.
On top of growing uncertainty
from the upcoming electoral cycle

and interest rate outlook, rising
competition and slowing demand
from China continue to challenge
key segments of the Consumer
Discretionary sector. And limited
direct AI concentration also leaves
Europe under-exposed to the key
growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and
emerging Asia.

Intervention from the ECB under its 2022 bond purchase program is also unlikely at this

stage given that the spread widening in France has not appeared either disorderly or
unwarranted. Meanwhile, conditions could potentially be aggravated by expected ECB rate
hikes in response to still above-target inflation across the eurozone. Indeed recent moves
to tighten monetary policy from a number of major central banks have been read most
hawkishly in Europe. Most of the increase in European bond yields has come from higher
inflation breakevens as opposed to real yields in the U.S. and Japan. And despite some

paring back of tightening expectations in the wake of recent bond volatility, the market is
still pricing three more policy rate increases over the next 12 months.
Euro depreciation and an emerging shift toward more protectionist trade policy with China
could further complicate the ECB policy outlook. China posted a record $35B trade surplus,
according to Eurostat, with the EU in September (alongside its $938B total surplus for the
first three quarters of 2026), and the two markets have agreed to reduce the EU bilateral

deficit by capping imports of hybrid cars for four years. Hybrids account for less than 2%
of total EU imports from China, but this could potentially mark the start of a broader shift
toward more trade protection in Europe with its largest foreign supplier which risks adding
further upward pressure to cost-push inflation. And though the leading candidates in the
French election may favor resuming oil and gas imports from Russia, Europe also remains

among the most at risk from the global energy shock. Indeed some 56% of the region’s
crude oil consumption is in the form of diesel product according to the International Energy
Agency, more than twice the U.S. share.
The 2- to 10-year eurozone yield curve has already flattened significantly since the start
of the year. And at just over 30 basis points as of mid-October, it remains flatter than
its equivalents in the U.S., UK and Japan. Outside Energy and Technology, Financials have

been among the strongest-performing sectors in Europe this year. But additional ECB
rate hikes could lead to further curve flattening, compressing bank net interest margins
and contributing to slower growth—particularly given that rate increases are coming in
response to supply-driven inflation rather than reflecting stronger economic activity.
We nonetheless continue to see pockets of strength in European markets. Growing
defense budgets should remain a tailwind for Industrials as national governments look to

meet their North Atlantic Treaty Organization targets over the next several years. And the
sector should be further supported by indirect exposure to AI investment through electric
cabling, power infrastructure and building materials for data centers. Leaders in renewable
energy equipment and services are also likely to be beneficiaries of grid upgrades and
broadening electrification globally, particularly given elevated fossil fuel prices. But our view

on the region remains cautious overall. On top of growing uncertainty from the upcoming
electoral cycle and interest rate outlook, rising competition and slowing demand from
China continue to challenge key segments of Europe’s Consumer Discretionary sector.
Limited direct AI concentration in technology hardware, cloud services and application
software also leaves Europe underexposed to the key growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and emerging Asia.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Cautious Optimism Entering Fiscal Year 2027
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
“A renewed focus on rebuilding fiscal space is urgent, given elevated debt, higher
borrowing costs, multiplying demands on public budgets and heightened uncertainty.” So
warned the IMF about the global fiscal outlook last week in its updated World Economic

Outlook report. Much is at stake for developed nations in Europe and Asia facing high
existing debt, unfavorable demographics and more modest growth.
For the U.S., between structurally higher mandatory outlays, the administration’s $1.5T
proposed defense package, and the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield sitting above 5%, spending
is likely headed higher. We continue to fade concerns of an impending U.S. debt crisis,
however, largely because economic growth continues to surprise to the upside. Expanding

output is good news for the debt-to-GDP ratio, which remains a touch below 100%.
Indeed, rather than being led higher on account of un-anchored inflation expectations, the
10-year U.S. Treasury yield has repriced to reflect better-than-expected nominal growth
(Exhibit 5A). Higher yields in the face of slowing growth, meanwhile, would be a different
story, not only for the fiscal outlook (worse) but also for U.S. equity valuations (lower).
Of course, after the U.S. accumulated another $2T deficit in FY 2026, 2 “rebuilding fiscal

space” in the IMF’s words will be politically unpopular and mathematically challenging.
In our view, the biggest near-term tail risk would be a surprise move lower in growth as
budget deficits at 6% of GDP in good times leave little room for bad times. This isn’t our
base case given robust consumer spending per our internal credit and debit card data and
ongoing investment in the AI buildout and beyond. We expect these two pillars to sustain
growth in 2027.

The other good news is that, contrary to concerns, demand for Treasurys remains robust.
Yields are attractive—that was the message from October’s successful $39B 10-year
Treasury note auction. At the same time, U.S. debt boasts an array of buyers: domestic
households, the Federal Reserve, mutual funds and exchange-traded funds, money market
funds, pension funds, foreign central banks, private investors overseas, and more. Per
Exhibit 5B, whereas foreign official investors once dominated some 40% of the U.S.

Treasury market during the 2008/2009 Great Financial Crisis, their share has fallen to
12% while domestic private investors now account for a majority of holdings. Translated:
demand is more diverse than most appreciate.

## Document 313: Cliff Notes_ mulling the minutes (2026-10-29)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ mulling the minutes (2026-10-29).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: mulling the minutes
By Sian Fenner, Ryan Wells & Illiana Jain
11:10 October 09 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
The only data release of note in Australia this week was the October Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment
Survey, which reported a 4.7% drop in the headline index. At 80.4, sentiment has tumbled back to
deeply pessimistic levels, similar to the post-pandemic cost-of-living shock when readings were among

the weakest in the survey’s more than fifty-year history. Indeed, inflation and interest rate pressures are
once again clearly front of mind for consumers, with both the view on ‘family finances vs a year ago’

### Page 2

and ‘family finance next 12 months’ falling 8.0% and 6.4% in the month, respectively. Moreover, the
RBA’s September rate hike looks to have had a significant impact – sentiment tanked 20% between
those surveyed before the RBA decision and those surveyed afterwards.

Views on the economic outlook were relatively more resilient, but they still weakened, with the one-year
and five-year ahead sub-indexes falling 1.6% and 0.4% respectively. Consumers are becoming more
‘on edge’ about job security, albeit not outright alarmed. The unemployment rate remains relatively low
versus history, although it has drifted up to a cycle high of 4.6% as labour supply continues to outstrip
demand. We expect it to hold there in next week’s September Labour Force Survey – see our preview
for more detail. All told, with higher interest rates, renewed inflation pressures and a softening jobs
market all weighing at once, households are entering the final months of the year on the back foot.

Offshore, the focus was on US data.

Before turning to this week's releases, it is worth noting that September non-farm payrolls came in
softer than expected across most metrics. Employment rose by only 29k (89k expected), and there
were sizeable downward revisions to prior months, with a combined 60k removed from July and August
payrolls, leaving gains of just 31k and 29k respectively. The details pointed to weaker hiring in leisure &
hospitality and education & health services industries, although construction and manufacturing
employment continued to benefit from ongoing data-centre-related investment. The unemployment rate
edged up to 4.2%, with softer employment occurring alongside a rise in labour force participation to
61.8%. Meanwhile wage growth moderated to 0.1%mth (3.0%yr). Taken together, the report suggests
labour demand is cooling, with the three-month average pace of job creation slowing to around 50k.
However, softer payroll gains are not necessarily indicative of labour market deterioration. Lower
immigration and ageing demographics have reduced the pace of employment growth needed to keep
the unemployment rate stable. As such, the report is more consistent with a labour market that is
gradually normalising than one entering a meaningful downturn.

The September FOMC minutes reinforced a distinctly hawkish message, with participants noting they
had "not seen sufficient progress on lowering inflation in recent months" and expressing concern that
inflation risks remained "skewed to the upside". Policymakers highlighted that both core services and
core goods inflation remained elevated, with some attributing persistent goods price strength to the
effects of the AI buildout despite the sector's traditional disinflationary role. There was also concern
that, after more than five years of inflation above target, elevated inflation could begin to influence
inflation expectations and wage- and price-setting behaviour. In contrast, labour market conditions
were generally viewed as stable and "close to maximum employment". While a majority noted the
labour market had strengthened somewhat recently, several participants highlighted unusually low
dynamism, characterised by low hiring, low layoffs, a low job-finding rate and elevated long-term
unemployment. Importantly, participants generally judged that aggregate wage growth was moderate
and "consistent with inflation moving toward 2 percent", with several explicitly noting that "the labor
market was not currently a source of inflationary pressures".

### Page 3

Related articles
A key focus was the interaction between AI investment, productive capacity and inflation. While
participants broadly expected AI-related investment to boost productivity growth and potential output
over time, several warned that "the AI buildout could cause aggregate demand to outpace aggregate
supply over the medium term, putting upward pressure on inflation". Consistent with this view, some
participants had raised their estimate of the neutral policy rate and several judged the current policy
stance to be "not restrictive or only mildly restrictive". Against this backdrop, all participants supported
the September rate increase, and most assessed that another increase in the federal funds rate would
likely be appropriate by year-end, although future decisions would remain data dependent.

The September ISM Services PMI released this week painted a similar picture of an economy that
remains resilient but is gradually losing momentum. The headline index eased to 54.9 from 55.4 in
August, with the decline driven by a sharp fall in business activity and a 9.4pt drop in new export
orders, which slipped into contractionary territory for the first time in eight months. Labour market
conditions improved modestly with the employment index moving to broadly neutral territory 50.1 after
two months below 50, supported by elevated backlogs and still-solid demand. Respondent
commentary illustrated the mixed nature of labour demand, with some firms reporting they were "filling
positions vacated due to promotions or retirements", while others cited "restructuring due to efficiencies
gained using AI tools". Cost pressures also remained elevated, with the prices paid index rising to 74.0,
its highest level since July 2022 and around 18pts above its historical average, while fuel costs, tariffs
and supply chain constraints remained the most frequently cited concerns. Overall, the report suggests
services activity remains consistent with ongoing expansion, albeit at a slower pace, while labour
demand and pricing pressures remain firm.

Overall, the data supports our view that the FOMC will move to a broadly neutral policy stance by the
end of the year with one more rate hike, although the case for a back-to-back move in October has
become less clear cut.

## Document 314: COLA comfort for older consumers_ Older households’ spending has lagged, but the 2027 Social Security cost-of-living adjustment (COLA) could help narrow the gap. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/COLA comfort for older consumers_ Older households’ spending has lagged, but the 2027 Social Security cost-of-living adjustment (COLA) could help narrow the gap. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13034011     1

Economy 
COLA comfort for older consumers? 
27 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Older generations' spending growth has lagged other cohorts over the last year in Bank of America aggregated credit and debit 
card data, despite sizeable wealth gains. This likely partly reflects the highly uneven distribution of wealth among older 
households, limiting the extent to which rising wealth supports spending across the cohort. 
• More recently, stronger consumer spending appears to be primarily a labor-income story. However, older households are less 
exposed to accelerating wage growth. In addition, large differences in their income sources help explain the considerable 
variation in spending among this cohort. 
• The recently announced 3.6% Social Security cost-of-living adjustment could provide a meaningful boost in 2027. If inflation 
moderates as expected, benefit increases may outpace price growth, supporting older households' purchasing power and 
potentially narrowing their spending gap with other generations.

Older generations’ spending growth has lagged  
While total credit and debit card spending per household has picked up in 2026 in Bank of America internal data, a notable 
laggard has been the spending growth of older generations (Baby Boomers and Traditionalists). For around a year, discretionary 
spending growth for these older generations has been lower than that of overall households. This is in stark contrast to 2022-
2023, when they were significantly outstripping overall household spending growth (Exhibit 1).  
Exhibit 1: Discretionary spending growth has been slower among older (Baby Boomer and Traditionalist) households despite wealth effects 
Total credit and debit card spending per household on discretionary categories by older generations and overall (three-month average, % YoY)

Source: Bank of America internal data

On one level, this observation seems surprising given that in our piece on the older consumer we noted that a large and growing 
proportion of net worth is held by older cohorts (read: Entering the age of the older consumer). So what’s going on? One 
explanation is that wealth is very unevenly distributed even within the older cohort. Exhibit 2 uses the latest Federal Reserve 
(2025) Survey of Consumer Finances to illustrate that, across households older than 65 years of age, around two-thirds of net 
wealth is held by the wealthiest 10% of households. As a result, wealth gains from rising stock markets may not trickle too far 
down the wealth distribution of older consumers.  
Exhibit 2: The wealth distribution for older households is very skewed  
Distribution of net wealth for households over 65 years old (%)

Source: Federal Reserve Board of Governors (2025 Survey of Consumer Finances)  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Income and spending become more diverse and unequal with age 
Another important part of the story is that the rise in overall spending growth over the past year appears to be supported by 
labor market strength as well as rising wealth. The October Consumer Checkpoint described how a pickup in after-tax wage 
growth among lower- and middle-income households was likely a key factor driving a recovery in these cohorts’ spending growth 
(read: Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers). As households age, their income sources become more diverse (Exhibit 3), 
which means older generations will likely benefit less from this pickup in wage growth.  
Exhibit 3: As households age their sources of income become more diverse  
Sources of income by age (%)

Source: Federal Reserve Board of Governors (2025 Survey of Household Economics and Decision making) 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

spending. For example, using Bank of America internal data, Exhibit 4 shows that households over age 62 that rely solely on

The 2027 COLA may offer some respite  
Still in our view there are some reasons for optimism around older generations spending outcomes.  
For some older households in the middle of the income distribution, tax policy is likely providing some support to their spending 
growth. The One Big Beautiful Bill Act introduced a temporary deduction of up to $6,000 for each taxpayer aged 65 or older for 
2025-2028, with the benefit phasing out above $75,000 of modified adjusted gross income for single filers and $150,000 for 
joint filers. This measure is likely lifting after-tax income and spending capacity for older households – particularly those in the 
middle of the income distribution.  
However, households on lower incomes may see less benefit from these policies as they likely already owe limited federal 
income tax. For these households relying on Social Security either as their sole or as a large component of their income, the 
forthcoming annual cost-of-living adjustment (COLA) to social security benefits will likely be a more important determinant of 
their budgets for 2027. The announced COLA for 2027 is a rise of 3.6% (Exhibit 7) – the largest since 2023, though lower than 
the 8.7% rise in 2022. 
Exhibit 7: The 2027 COLA increase is the largest since 2023 and close to the historical average  
Social Security cost of living adjustments (% increase as of January effective date)

Source: US Social Security Administration (SSA)   
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
The COLA increases are based on the year-over-year (YoY) % change between the third quarters of successive years in the 
Consumer Price Index for Urban Wage Earners and Clerical Workers (CPI-W) and are typically announced in October and effective 
the following January. If the COLA increase simply keeps benefits in line with inflation, why is it particularly interesting? For sure, 
many times it is not. But when inflation is close to a turning point, the increase can end up looking relatively high or low 
compared with inflation over the course of the following year. The 2022 COLA increase was a case in point, as inflation in the 
subsequent year slowed sharply. In our view, this was one reason older generations’ spending growth was stronger than that of 
other age cohorts over 2022-2023.   
Exhibit 8: Inflation is expected to decline next year 
Consumer Price Index (CPI) (% YoY), third quarter CPI-W increases (red dots, % YoY) and latest consensus CPI forecast for 2027 (dotted line, % YoY)

Source: Haver Analytics, Bloomberg. Some fall 2025 months might be missing due to the government shutdown.  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Regarding the 2027 COLA announcement, there are some parallels with 2022, albeit on a smaller scale. In particular, the 
consensus expectation is that CPI inflation is likely to cool over 2027, largely reflecting the lessening impact of higher gasoline 
prices (Exhibit 8).  
So if these expectations prove correct, households that rely on Social Security may receive a more impactful boost to their 
budgets next year than they have for the last few, potentially narrowing the gap between older generations’ spending and other 
households.  
What categories of spending growth might benefit from this? It is hard to be sure, but notable growth deficits for older 
generations currently reside in discretionary areas like electronics, lodging and restaurants (Exhibit 9). Some of these may reflect 
the preferences of older households, but it could be that there is room for some narrowing in these areas.  
Exhibit 9: Large spending growth gaps for older households exist in a range of discretionary goods and services

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Store category aggregation for “more discretionary”, “partly discretionary”, and “mostly necessity” is defined as follows: 
More discretionary: furniture, electronics, building materials, hobbies, miscellaneous stores, nonstore retail, clothing. 
Partly discretionary: auto, health and personal care, general merchandise. 
Mostly necessity: Gasoline, food and beverage.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts. 
The “more discretionary” retail category includes furniture, electronics, building materials, online marketplaces, clothing retailers and 
miscellaneous stores; while “partly discretionary” retail contains auto parts and services, health and personal care retailers, general merchandise 
and “mostly necessity”  retail contains gas and groceries. 
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Bank of America Proprietary Market Landscape Insights Study is an online quantitative survey among Bank of America customers and 
noncustomers sampled and balanced to provide a representative view of the U.S. adult population. Insights are based on aggregated and 
anonymized responses to surveys. Significance testing is done at the 90 percent confidence interval. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

Are investors rational?
The stock market strength and resilience over the last 3 months appears to be a rational response to strong
earnings and only limited signs that higher energy prices are materially affecting broad corporate profits.
Our dbLumina gauge indicates investors have been irrational during market

drops and periods of geopolitical tensions What does it mean to be ‘rational’?
Risk-off periods:
The tariff fears in 2025 and then the Iran
war in 2026 were key buying opportunities
in equity markets. Yet, the overwhelming
fear exhibited by investors in these periods
largely meant they sold off risk assets in an
emotional and irrational response.
Risk-on periods:
It can be rational to buy into a rising
market. Our indicator suggests this is what
happened in Q3 as earnings growth

continued to be strong.

threats, widespread sell-offs on

beyond what might be fundamentally

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging risks underestimated by the market – part 1
A reallocation of global sovereign reserve assets is accelerating, spurred by
geopolitical uncertainty and the fear of trade and financial sanctions. Notably,
central banks are repatriating their physical assets, such as the Dutch and French

central banks which have recently moved gold reserves out of New York.
Furthermore, a World Gold Council survey reveals that 45% of global central banks
plan to increase their gold reserves over the next 12 months.
What the market underestimates: The market views the recent surges in gold prices
as temporary, cyclical safe-haven flows. In reality, sovereign institutions are
structurally preparing for a more fragmented global trade system where tariff

policies and sanctions may make dollar-clearing assets more difficult.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Interest rates: At a time when the "world is awash in debt", long-term interest rates
of some countries may be forced structurally higher to attract alternative private
capital.
Inflation: Unexpected dollar depreciation may increase the cost of imported goods
Equity Prices: High-growth US equity valuations, which are highly sensitive to the

long-term risk-free rate, may face multiple compression if bond yields are pushed
higher.
The market currently prices in a material, long-term GDP boost from AI-driven
productivity gains. However, some research from the IMF suggests that over-reliance
on standardised LLMs can produce a high degree of "sameness" and uniformity in
cognitive output. Furthermore, escalating intellectual property and copyright
litigation threaten to bifurcate the quality of AI models based strictly on developers'

licensing budgets.
What the market underestimates: Some investors assume that the exponential
curve of AI hardware capabilities translates directly into exponential economic
output. But there is a risk that if AI tools merely standardise corporate processes
rather than innovate them, and legal barriers restrict access to premium data, the
productivity boom may be less aggressive than expected.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
GDP growth: Structural growth ceiling if AI fails to deliver the multi-percentage

point boost to global productivity required to offset aging demographics.
Equity prices: A downward revaluation of the technology and software sector if
massive AI infrastructure expenditures fail to generate revenue according to existing
expectations.
Geopolitical fragmentation of global reserve assets The AI “sameness“ trap and the productivity stagnation1 2
8

### Page 10

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging risks underestimated by the market – part 2
Advanced economies are facing a dual demographic crisis: rapidly aging populations
and underfunded pension systems. This is forcing some older people to remain in or
re-enter the workforce out of financial necessity rather than choice.

What the market underestimates: Lower unemployment rates are celebrated as a
sign of economic strength. However, in structurally ageing societies like Japan and
parts of Europe, the growth of the labour force is increasingly driven by part-time,
older workers. Furthermore, G20 nations lack adequate, cohesive policy frameworks
to address these long-term demographic fiscal drains.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Inflation: An older workforce working out of necessity has less discretionary

spending power, potentially locking economies into persistent, structural demand-
side deflationary forces, offset by supply-side wage pressures in highly skilled
sectors.
GDP growth: Labour productivity growth that is lower than it might otherwise be as
the median age of the workforce climbs, limiting long-term potential GDP.
The geopolitical battleground is focussed on energy blockades (such as the Strait of
Hormuz) but underestimates the weaponisation of critical minerals. China currently

holds ‘escalation dominance’ over rare-earth mineral supply chains. The emerging
risk is the proactive restriction of these minerals to Western firms, such as recent
bans on supplying rare-earth inputs to some US firms.
What the market underestimates: The market treats rare-earth supply constraints as
temporary trade spat negotiation tactics. In reality, as Western nations aggressively
push for reshoring and friend-shoring, China is increasingly using its processing

monopoly as a long-term strategic blockade to try to slow Western high-tech,
defence and the energy transition.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Inflation: Upwards pressure on inflation as the cost of manufacturing electric
vehicles, semiconductors, and advanced electronics surges due to supply blockades
and the high capital cost of rebuilding localised processing facilities.
Interest rates: Central banks may be forced to keep policy rates higher to combat

the persistent supply-side inflation generated by supply-chain fragmentation.
The fragmentation of the EU’s political centre Critical mineral isolationism and ‘green’ supply chain blockades3 4
9

### Page 11

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging themes – part 1
Our dbLumina AI model helps us identify the key emerging themes that have the potential to
most impact markets and economies in the short term
While the previous focus was on the AI hardware squeeze and
commercial competition, the narrative is shifting towards AI's role as a

critical component of national security and geopolitical leverage.
— AI access: We see this in President Trump's framing of AI development as a
geopolitical race ("whoever wins AI wins").
— AI as a bargaining chip: Several examples over the last 12 months including the
2025 deal whereby AMD agreed to pay 15% of sales from certain AI chips to the
US government in exchange for an export license. Even if these deals change, the
trajectory is one where access to AI technology is tied to national economic and

security interests.
— Direct military & security applications: Several examples including, cyberattacks
on critical infrastructure (such as a UK power plant), the use of AI in developing
"exceptional" phages to fight E.coli, and the growing field of AI security
discussions.
Outlook: Expect more government intervention, the use of AI technology access as a
tool in diplomatic negotiations, and a greater focus on the dual-use nature of AI in
both commercial and military contexts. This will likely create regulatory and

operational uncertainty for tech firms.
The trend of reshoring is evolving into a more proactive diplomatic
strategy where nations are forming new, often transactional, alliances
to navigate a world less dominated by a single superpower.
— Proactive alliance building: Canadian Prime Minister Carney has been front and
centre making his first international trip to France and the UK in an effort to
diversify away from traditional reliance on the US. That follows other key

meetings between Japan and India, and more.
— Bypassing traditional powers: Greenland signalled its willingness to turn to
China for investment if Western countries do not provide support, reinforcing the
leverage of some smaller nations.
— Shifting security arrangements: The US pushed for a ‘reassurance force’ in
Ukraine, while French President Macron hosted a "coalition of the willing" to
discuss Europe's defence without US guarantees. Further examples include the

discussion of a Franco-German "nuclear sharing" arrangement.
Outlook: The assumptions of a bipolar world order is giving way to a more complex,
multi-nodal system. This will create greater emphasis on regional blocs and issue-
specific coalitions. The stability of traditional alliances like NATO and the G7 remain
deeply uncertain.
AI’s impact on geopolitics and national security The fracturing of multilateralism and the rise of "Middle Power Countries" 1 2

10

### Page 12

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging themes – part 2
These four key themes dbLumina identifies are sometimes ‘known’ by investors, however, they may be underestimated by the market,
particularly when there are some potentially series longer-term impacts
Previous talk of “sovereign fiscal fragility” has now manifested itself

into a severe market reaction. Although fears around government debt
and deficits pop up from time to time, the magnitude of September’s
bond sell-off has been compounded by oil price concerns
— Accelerating sovereign sell-off: Concerns about inflation and debt sustainability
are not new but the peaks in bond yields are. Yields on 10-year Treasuries, bunds,
and gilts all rose sharply, with bunds hitting their highest level since 2009.
— Fiscal policy as a direct market catalyst: The market is increasingly sensitive to

fiscal policy announcements. For example, in the UK postponed tax rises, led to a
spike in gilt yields. Similarly, France's political turmoil and concerns over its
budget deficit caused the Franco-German yield spread to widen significantly.
— The “awash in debt" narrative: Treasury Secretary Bessent's comment at the G20
that "The world is awash in debt" signalled that the issue is increasingly top-of-
mind for policymakers.
Outlook: Markets will be increasingly quick to punish any perceived fiscal

irresponsibility. Expect higher borrowing costs, currency volatility, and increased risk
of financial instability, especially for countries with high debt levels.
The strain in the US consumer and labour markets is now diverging
sharply from the health of large corporations, reinforcing the K-shaped
economy.
— Strong corporate performance: Earnings season for Q2 showed remarkably solid
global growth, and the resilience of large corporations was a recurring theme

that helped support equity markets despite other headwinds.
— Weak consumer & labour signals: Economic reports have showed some signs of
a weakening labour market, falling consumer confidence, and stress among low-
income Americans. The New York Fed's survey also showed households expect a
worse financial situation.
— Policy and market disconnect: While strong corporate performance depends on
a robust economy, the underlying consumer weakness is concerning. Expect the

market to continue its twitchy reaction to data, as investors struggle to reconcile
these conflicting signals.
Outlook: This theme is potentially the most consequential for the domestic US
economy. If corporate profits and equity markets "detach" from the real economy as
consumers perceive it, it could lead to significant social and political instability,
making economic policy even more difficult to navigate.
The return of global bond vigilantes The bifurcation of economies: corporate resilience vs. consumer strain3 4

the index to fall. Rather it means we see the rising probability that returns will be lower than previously expected.

弘塑 3131 TT  ( 電子上游–設備 ) 
 ) 
訂單能見度無虞 
投資與評價 
1. 弘塑 2Q26 毛利率僅達 38.9%， 低於我們原先及市場預期的40%水準，
主要受到添鴻產品結構轉差及新廠折舊攤提影響。 
2. 3Q26 受惠於 CoWoS 等機台認列數成長及強勁拉貨需求，營收達
22.21 億元；研究部預估 EPS 為 15.86 元。 
3. FY26 全年出機量預估維持在 220 至 240 台，展望至 FY27/FY28，
FY27 設備出貨量預估將年增 50%，FY28 亦有機會再成長 50%。 
4. 研究部預估 FY26/27 EPS 分別為 60.66 元/96.33 元，維持弘塑買進投
資建議，目標價 2,500 元，以 26x FY27 獲利預估。 
重點更新 
至 FY28 出貨量能將持續成長 
研究部認為弘塑設備出貨展望能見度將能延伸至 FY28。 由於晶圓廠 、OSAT、
記憶體客戶皆對先進封裝設備需求相當迫切，設備交期已長達 10 至 12 個
月，惟受限於短期人力供應與廠房籌備進度， FY26 全年出機量預估維持在 
220 至 240 台。展望至 FY27/FY28，FY27 設備出貨量預估將年增 50%，
FY28 亦有機會再成長 50%。 
添鴻化學品業務將於 1Q27 逐漸看見轉機 
隨 OSAT 客戶於今年 Q2/Q3 進入裝機密集期，新產線仍須經測試與量產爬
坡，相關的化學品需求通常會在設備交機後約 3~4 個月逐步提升，因此短期
營收仍受新廠折舊與稼動率偏低影響，預期添鴻的整體稼動率與毛利率有望
在 1Q27 逐漸擺脫谷底，迎來較為明顯的營運轉機。 
訂單能見度無虞 
展望 2026 年至 2028 年 ， 在各先進封裝平台強勁需求下 ， 設備產能已告全
滿，交期長達 10 至 12 個月，訂單能見度直達 2H27。就產品組合來看，
設備業務高階產品比重將逐漸提升，預估至 FY27 SoIC /CPO 等營收占比將
提升至 10~15%，添鴻化學品也將受惠於 OSAT 客戶逐漸提升拉貨動能，整
體營收與獲利成長動能明確。研究部預估 FY26/27 EPS 分別為 60.66 元
/96.33 元(YoY＋33.5%/+58.8%)。弘塑目前交易於 35/22 倍之 2026/2027 
年獲利預估。研究部維持買進投資建議，目標價 2,500 元，以 26x FY27 獲
利預估。

### Page 2

訪談內容 
3Q26 受惠於機台認列數成長，營收呈現季增 
弘塑 2Q26 營收 17.27 億元 (QoQ +8.2% / YoY +5.9%)，符合我們原先預估的 16.88 億元；毛
利率僅達 38.9% (QoQ +5.1ppts / YoY -1.7ppts)，低於我們原先及市場預期的 40%水準，主要
受到添鴻產品結構轉差及新廠折舊攤提影響 ；EPS 為 11.18 元 (QoQ -30.6% / YoY +58.6%)，
略低於我們原先預期的 11.73 元。 
3Q26 受惠於 CoWoS 等機台認列數成長及強勁拉貨需求，營收呈現季增，營收達 22.21 億元
(QoQ +28.7%/YoY+48.7%) ；研究部預估毛利率 略為季增至 40.1% (QoQ + 1.2ppts / YoY 
-0.5ppts)，產品組合持續改善，預估 EPS 為 15.86 元(QoQ +41.9%/ YoY +52.5%)。 
展望 4Q26， 受惠於先進封裝及記憶體等強勁需求的持續出機認列，研究部預期營收將持續季增，
預估 4Q26 營收將達 24.87 億元(QoQ +12.0%/YoY+15.7%)，毛利率將持穩於 40.0% (QoQ 
-0.1ppts / YoY -2.1ppts)。整體而言，預估 FY26 全年營收達 80.32 億元(YoY+23.3%)，毛利率
達 38.5%(YoY-2.6 ppts)，EPS 為 60.66 元(YoY+33.5%)。
